like head injury or alcohol).\n  manifestation: I can remember some parts of what happened very clearly, like the contractor’s face and the noise, but there’s a chunk in the middle where I know I was there and I just can’t pull it back.\n- key: S074\n  name: Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State\n  description: Persistent negative emotional state (e.g., fear, horror, anger, guilt, or shame).\n ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I guess there is something specific, yeah. I’m just… I don’t like calling it traumatic, because that sounds dramatic, and I don’t want to make it seem bigger than it was.

But I do avoid parts of it. Like at work, I’ll take the long way around the building so I don’t have to pass that area, and I avoid that floor whenever I can. And if people start talking about the contractor or the cutover, I kind of shut the conversation down or find a reason to leave.

I know that probably sounds like avoidance. I’m not trying to be difficult—I just don’t really want to go back into it if I don’t have to.

I’m not trying to be difficult—I just don’t really want to go back into it if I don’t have to.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about the late-night cutover incident you mentioned earlier?\n\nPatient: I’m sorry, I don’t think I can give you much more detail than what I already said. I know I mentioned it, but I’m still kind of… vague on how to talk about it without making it sound worse than it was.\n\nIt was a work thing, late at night, and it had to do with that area and the cutover. That’s really about as specific as I feel comfortable being right now, if that’s okay. I’m not trying to avoid your question exactly—I just don’t know how much is useful to say.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel disconnected from yourself or your surroundings when you're not acting on impulses?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I don’t think so, not in a way I would know how to name.\n\nSometimes I might sort of zone out or feel a little distant if I’m stressed, but I’m not sure that’s what you mean. It doesn’t feel like I’m outside my body or anything dramatic like that. Maybe I just get quiet or go on autopilot a bit.\n\nIs that the kind of thing you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Did you often get into trouble or act out when you were a teenager?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I might get distracted or kind of go quiet sometimes, but I wouldn’t say I’m in a daze exactly.\n\nMaybe I’m not recognizing it the way you mean it, though. When you say spaced out, do you mean like losing track of what’s happening around me, or just feeling kind of mentally checked out?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're not really yourself when you're acting on these impulses?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’d put it that way, no. When I do something impulsive, I might feel embarrassed afterward or wonder why I did it, but in the moment it still feels like me, I guess.\n\nI’m not sure if that’s the kind of “not yourself” you mean. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're in a daze or spaced out when you're not acting on impulses?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not really. I mean, I might get distracted or kind of go quiet sometimes, but I wouldn’t say I’m in a daze exactly.\n\nMaybe I’m not recognizing it the way you mean it, though. When you say spaced out, do you mean like losing track of what’s happening around me, or just feeling kind of mentally checked out?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're not really yourself when you're acting on these impulses?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’d put it that way, no. When I do something impulsive, I might feel embarrassed afterward or wonder why I did it, but in the moment it still feels like me, I guess.\n\nI’m not sure if that’s the kind of “not yourself” you mean. I don’t feel like a different person takes over or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Did you have any problems with rules or authority figures when you were younger?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I mean, I tried to be respectful, especially with teachers and adults. I wasn’t someone who was getting suspended or arguing with authority all the time.\n\nI probably had moments where I didn’t like being told what to do, like any kid, but nothing major that I can think of. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Did you have any significant behavioral problems before you turned 15?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I wasn’t getting into serious trouble or anything like that before 15.\n\nI mean, I’m sure I had normal kid moments, but nothing I’d call significant behavioral problems. At least not that I remember. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like there's a part of you that's observing your impulsive actions, but not controlling them?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’ve noticed it that way. Like, I can sometimes think afterward, “Why did I do that?” but in the moment I don’t really feel like there’s a separate part of me watching it happen.\n\nI don’t know if that makes sense. I don’t want to overstate it if it’s not what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Did you often get into physical fights or bully others when you were younger?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I wasn’t the kind of kid who got into fights or picked on people.\n\nI mean, maybe there were little arguments or normal school stuff, but I don’t remember bullying anyone or being physically aggressive. I hope that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific about the late-night cutover incident that you're avoiding talking about?\n\nPatient: I guess… yes, a little. I don’t mean to be difficult about it.\n\nI just try not to go near that part of the building if I can help it. I’ll take the longer way around, or avoid that floor unless I absolutely have to. And when people start talking about the contractor or the cutover, I kind of shut it down or change the subject.\n\nIt’s probably not the best way to handle it, but it’s what I’ve been doing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your impulsive behaviors are part of a long-standing pattern of how you interact with the world, rather than a response to a specific stressful event?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think it’s a long-standing pattern. At least, I really hope it doesn’t come across that way.\n\nI came in because I’ve started doing things that scare me a little, like walking out in the middle of deployments or driving around late at night with no real plan. That’s… not how I’ve ever handled stress before. Usually I’m pretty controlled, maybe too controlled.\n\nSo I think it feels more tied to what happened than just “how I am,” if that makes sense."
